knowledge_library/t39/SOURCE_PINS.md:20:## S1: tree-martingale-family boundary candidate: flows and derived trees
knowledge_library/t39/SOURCE_PINS.md:22:Russell Lyons and Yuval Peres, *Probability on Trees and Networks*, Cambridge
knowledge_library/t39/SOURCE_PINS.md:23:University Press, 2016, corrected online edition dated 2020.
knowledge_library/t39/SOURCE_PINS.md:25:- DOI: <https://doi.org/10.1017/9781316672815>
knowledge_library/t39/SOURCE_PINS.md:26:- Author page: <https://rdlyons.pages.iu.edu/prbtree/>
knowledge_library/t39/SOURCE_PINS.md:27:- Retrieved PDF URL: <https://rdlyons.pages.iu.edu/prbtree/book_corr.pdf>
knowledge_library/t39/SOURCE_PINS.md:28:- Retained PDF: `lyons-peres-2020-corrected.pdf`
knowledge_library/t39/SOURCE_PINS.md:29:- PDF SHA-256: `3ba07bc0fb0397dc256610b328c869983d7ab4f709c78952d86646e25a15d043`
knowledge_library/t39/SOURCE_PINS.md:30:- Retained extract: `lyons-peres-2020-corrected.txt`
knowledge_library/t39/SOURCE_PINS.md:31:- Extract SHA-256: `3f734a0413e5250be19bb227bcae28aec26350eb878101d03ba1496954aaad7a`
knowledge_library/t39/SOURCE_PINS.md:32:- Exact locators: corrected printed p. 526, extract lines 27403--27419 defines
knowledge_library/t39/SOURCE_PINS.md:33:  descendant trees, conditional flows, and `D(T)`; corrected printed p. 528,
knowledge_library/t39/SOURCE_PINS.md:34:  extract lines 27473--27492 states Theorem 15.20; its construction begins at
knowledge_library/t39/SOURCE_PINS.md:35:  extract lines 27493--27506.
knowledge_library/t39/SOURCE_PINS.md:37:The retained theorem assumes a uniformly bounded-degree rooted tree. In the
knowledge_library/t39/SOURCE_PINS.md:38:section's standing setup it is represented as a rooted subtree of a fixed
knowledge_library/t39/SOURCE_PINS.md:39:`r`-ary tree, with no leaves except possibly the root. Theorem 15.20 produces
knowledge_library/t39/SOURCE_PINS.md:40:`T* in D(T)` and a unit flow on `T*` with the displayed boundary-dimension and
knowledge_library/t39/SOURCE_PINS.md:41:flow-exponent properties. The relevant operation is explicitly recentering:
knowledge_library/t39/SOURCE_PINS.md:42:`T^v` identifies `v` with a new root and `theta^v(x)=theta(x)/theta(v)`.
knowledge_library/t39/SOURCE_PINS.md:44:This is the retained candidate for the requested tree-martingale family
knowledge_library/t39/SOURCE_PINS.md:45:because its source-pinned conditional-flow operation is the tree-filtration
knowledge_library/t39/SOURCE_PINS.md:46:conditioning mechanism relevant to moving roots. The theorem itself is a
knowledge_library/t39/SOURCE_PINS.md:47:derived-tree/unit-flow theorem, not a martingale convergence theorem; no claim
knowledge_library/t39/SOURCE_PINS.md:48:that generic martingale convergence creates or anchors a branch is made.
knowledge_library/t39/SOURCE_PINS.md:50:## S2: quasi-Bernoulli Gibbs measure
knowledge_library/t39/SOURCE_PINS.md:52:De-Jun Feng and Ka-Sing Lau, "The Pressure Function for Products of
knowledge_library/t39/SOURCE_PINS.md:53:Non-negative Matrices", *Mathematical Research Letters* 9 (2002), 363--378.
knowledge_library/t39/SOURCE_PINS.md:55:- DOI: <https://doi.org/10.4310/MRL.2002.v9.n3.a10>
knowledge_library/t39/SOURCE_PINS.md:56:- Publisher record: <https://link.intlpress.com/JDetail/1806606366691192834>
knowledge_library/t39/SOURCE_PINS.md:57:- Retrieved PDF URL:
knowledge_library/t39/SOURCE_PINS.md:58:  <https://www.intlpress.com/site/pub/files/_fulltext/journals/mrl/2002/0009/0003/MRL-2002-0009-0003-a010.pdf>
knowledge_library/t39/SOURCE_PINS.md:59:- Retained PDF: `feng-lau-2002.pdf`
knowledge_library/t39/SOURCE_PINS.md:60:- PDF SHA-256: `1ead52b41ef41987ae07067b06fb83d0f5196c40a4e88d7c3c9524ab8c3e7bec`
knowledge_library/t39/SOURCE_PINS.md:61:- Retained extract: `feng-lau-2002.txt`
knowledge_library/t39/SOURCE_PINS.md:62:- Extract SHA-256: `5fe33cd079e2e54b488a7d37e193975d1f769ad5a72d850670461e87b782563e`
knowledge_library/t39/SOURCE_PINS.md:63:- Exact locators: printed p. 363, extract lines 25--43 fixes the primitive
knowledge_library/t39/SOURCE_PINS.md:64:  subshift and cylinder notation; Theorem 1.1 is printed p. 364, extract lines
knowledge_library/t39/SOURCE_PINS.md:65:  80--97; the two-sided quasi-Bernoulli estimate (1.4) is printed p. 365,
knowledge_library/t39/SOURCE_PINS.md:66:  extract lines 103--109; Corollary 2.6 and proof are printed p. 370, extract
knowledge_library/t39/SOURCE_PINS.md:67:  lines 369--380.
knowledge_library/t39/SOURCE_PINS.md:69:The hypotheses are one primitive finite-type shift, a Holder-continuous
knowledge_library/t39/SOURCE_PINS.md:70:strictly positive matrix-valued function, and fixed `q in R`. Theorem 1.1
knowledge_library/t39/SOURCE_PINS.md:71:produces one invariant ergodic Gibbs probability measure. Equation (1.4)
knowledge_library/t39/SOURCE_PINS.md:72:states one word-independent constant comparison
knowledge_library/t39/SOURCE_PINS.md:74:`C^-1 mu([I]) mu([J]) <= mu([IJ]) <= C mu([I]) mu([J])`
knowledge_library/t39/SOURCE_PINS.md:76:for every admissible concatenation. Corollary 2.6's displayed first inequality
knowledge_library/t39/SOURCE_PINS.md:77:at extract line 371 appears reversed relative to (1.4), its proof, and the
knowledge_library/t39/SOURCE_PINS.md:78:standard meaning stated immediately afterward. This audit retains (1.4) as the
knowledge_library/t39/SOURCE_PINS.md:79:unambiguous exact display and records rather than silently repairs the later
knowledge_library/t39/SOURCE_PINS.md:80:typesetting issue.
knowledge_library/t39/SOURCE_PINS.md:82:## S3: tangent distributions
knowledge_library/t39/SOURCE_PINS.md:84:Antti Kaenmaki, Tuomas Sahlsten, and Pablo Shmerkin, "Dynamics of the scenery
knowledge_library/t39/SOURCE_PINS.md:85:flow and geometry of measures", *Proceedings of the London Mathematical
knowledge_library/t39/SOURCE_PINS.md:86:Society* (3) 110 (2015), 1248--1280.
knowledge_library/t39/SOURCE_PINS.md:88:- DOI: <https://doi.org/10.1112/plms/pdv003>
knowledge_library/t39/SOURCE_PINS.md:89:- arXiv record: <https://arxiv.org/abs/1401.0231v3>
knowledge_library/t39/SOURCE_PINS.md:90:- Retrieved PDF URL: <https://arxiv.org/pdf/1401.0231v3>
knowledge_library/t39/SOURCE_PINS.md:91:- Retained PDF: `kaenmaki-sahlsten-shmerkin-2015.pdf`
knowledge_library/t39/SOURCE_PINS.md:92:- PDF SHA-256: `6e17b161355633d4293ad2428550cbc7b919f8ee3fbfa4adb36970d5daa5783f`
knowledge_library/t39/SOURCE_PINS.md:93:- Retained extract: `kaenmaki-sahlsten-shmerkin-2015.txt`
knowledge_library/t39/SOURCE_PINS.md:94:- Extract SHA-256: `b37f8b34299c37e0acb4d7010522becf8e83c64520f9e1f07a27523d3a97f64d`
knowledge_library/t39/SOURCE_PINS.md:95:- Exact locators: arXiv PDF pp. 14--16, extract lines 689--736 gives
knowledge_library/t39/SOURCE_PINS.md:96:  Definitions 3.1, 3.2, and 3.4; Definition 3.7 is extract lines 748--767;
knowledge_library/t39/SOURCE_PINS.md:97:  Theorem 3.10 is arXiv PDF p. 16 (journal p. 1263), extract lines 795--802.
knowledge_library/t39/SOURCE_PINS.md:99:Theorem 3.10 says that for any Radon measure `mu`, at `mu`-almost every point,
knowledge_library/t39/SOURCE_PINS.md:100:all tangent distributions are fractal distributions. These are time-averaged
knowledge_library/t39/SOURCE_PINS.md:101:scenery limits, not individual tangent measures and not paths. The source
knowledge_library/t39/SOURCE_PINS.md:102:normalizes each scenery to a probability measure and proves scale invariance
knowledge_library/t39/SOURCE_PINS.md:103:and quasi-Palm behavior; it does not retain an absolute tree root.
knowledge_library/t39/SOURCE_PINS.md:105:## S4: symbolic languages and subshifts
knowledge_library/t39/SOURCE_PINS.md:107:Jorge Almeida and Alfredo Costa, "A geometric interpretation of the
knowledge_library/t39/SOURCE_PINS.md:108:Schutzenberger group of a minimal subshift", *Arkiv for Matematik* 54 (2016),
knowledge_library/t39/SOURCE_PINS.md:109:243--275.
knowledge_library/t39/SOURCE_PINS.md:111:- DOI: <https://doi.org/10.1007/s11512-016-0233-7>
knowledge_library/t39/SOURCE_PINS.md:112:- arXiv record: <https://arxiv.org/abs/1507.06885>
knowledge_library/t39/SOURCE_PINS.md:113:- Retrieved PDF URL: <https://arxiv.org/pdf/1507.06885>
knowledge_library/t39/SOURCE_PINS.md:114:- Retained PDF: `almeida-costa-2016.pdf`
knowledge_library/t39/SOURCE_PINS.md:115:- PDF SHA-256: `f774b8a9df74809038e95b4925867e99673f2fb055e4568e507a67bb0f2c1ce4`
knowledge_library/t39/SOURCE_PINS.md:116:- Retained extract: `almeida-costa-2016.txt`
knowledge_library/t39/SOURCE_PINS.md:117:- Extract SHA-256: `57b1e48172ea02ab285dfafd9427373e28036720311e86a82cf0f4054deafc24`
knowledge_library/t39/SOURCE_PINS.md:118:- Exact locator: open PDF pp. 7--8, journal pp. 249--250, extract lines
knowledge_library/t39/SOURCE_PINS.md:119:  359--380 defines the finite alphabet, subshift, block language, and states
knowledge_library/t39/SOURCE_PINS.md:120:  that `X -> L(X)` is an isomorphism between subshifts and factorial,
knowledge_library/t39/SOURCE_PINS.md:121:  prolongable languages. The paper attributes this correspondence to Lind and
knowledge_library/t39/SOURCE_PINS.md:122:  Marcus, Proposition 1.3.4.
knowledge_library/t39/SOURCE_PINS.md:124:The theorem concerns one fixed finite alphabet and one fixed factorial,
knowledge_library/t39/SOURCE_PINS.md:125:prolongable language. Its resulting point lies in a shift-coordinate space;
knowledge_library/t39/SOURCE_PINS.md:126:shifting an occurrence to coordinate zero does not retain an external absolute
knowledge_library/t39/SOURCE_PINS.md:127:start depth or any cylinder mass.

knowledge_library/t105/SOURCE_PINS.md:71:- Bryce Kerr, *Incomplete exponential sums over exponential functions*,
knowledge_library/t105/SOURCE_PINS.md:72:  `Quarterly Journal of Mathematics` 66 (2015), 213-224.

knowledge_library/t105/SOURCE_PINS.md:81:  Theorem 2 is the retained incomplete-orbit bound and requires prime `p`,
knowledge_library/t105/SOURCE_PINS.md:82:  `g` of order `t`, and `N<=t`. Its displayed first branch
knowledge_library/t105/SOURCE_PINS.md:83:  `p^(1/8)N^(71/96+o(1))` additionally requires `N<=t^(1/2)`; layout extract
knowledge_library/t105/SOURCE_PINS.md:84:  lines 78-87.
knowledge_library/t105/SOURCE_PINS.md:85:- Date exception: this 2015 paper is outside the preferred 2020-2026 window,
knowledge_library/t105/SOURCE_PINS.md:86:  but it was retained because it is the closest direct theorem for incomplete
knowledge_library/t105/SOURCE_PINS.md:87:  fixed-residue sums `sum_(j<N) e_p(lambda g^j)` found in the bounded search.

knowledge_library/t105/SOURCE_PINS.md:111:primary paper was screened at abstract, theorem, or full-text level. Discovery
knowledge_library/t105/SOURCE_PINS.md:112:queries used arXiv/DOI metadata and the phrases `energy sum-product`,

knowledge_library/t105/SOURCE_PINS.md:114:exponential sums powers`, and `multiplicative subgroup exponential sums`, but
knowledge_library/t105/SOURCE_PINS.md:115:unopened search-result titles are not treated as inspected primary sources and
knowledge_library/t105/SOURCE_PINS.md:116:support no claim in this report. T103 and T104 were consulted only as local
knowledge_library/t105/SOURCE_PINS.md:117:prior-fingerprint reports under the separately enumerated pins below. This
knowledge_library/t105/SOURCE_PINS.md:118:exhaustive ledger makes `PRIMARY_SOURCE_COUNT: 5` the searched-source count as
knowledge_library/t105/SOURCE_PINS.md:119:well as the retained-source count. No claim of an exhaustive global literature
knowledge_library/t105/SOURCE_PINS.md:120:review or novelty is made.

knowledge_library/t149/SOURCE_PINS.md:90:- Delivered extract: `montgomery-vaughan-1973.txt`
knowledge_library/t149/SOURCE_PINS.md:91:- Extract SHA-256:
knowledge_library/t149/SOURCE_PINS.md:92:  `c1be79a7b3f13f6496aff93727b19bfbb2b63e1226ee5c352524e56beb631c31`
knowledge_library/t149/SOURCE_PINS.md:93:- Exact locator: printed p. 119, equations (1.1), (1.3), Theorem 1 and equation
knowledge_library/t149/SOURCE_PINS.md:94:  (1.4), extract lines 10-46. For arbitrary coefficients on an interval of
knowledge_library/t149/SOURCE_PINS.md:95:  length `N` and frequencies separated modulo one by `delta`, the sum of
knowledge_library/t149/SOURCE_PINS.md:96:  squared exponential sums is strictly below
knowledge_library/t149/SOURCE_PINS.md:97:  `(N+delta^(-1))*sum |a_n|^2`.
knowledge_library/t149/SOURCE_PINS.md:98:- Screen: decimal labels `b/10^m` have spacing only `10^(-m)`, producing the
knowledge_library/t149/SOURCE_PINS.md:99:  exponentially large `delta^(-1)=10^m` term. Repeated labels must first be
knowledge_library/t149/SOURCE_PINS.md:100:  combined into coefficients, so the theorem starts with, rather than bounds,
knowledge_library/t149/SOURCE_PINS.md:101:  their squared multiplicity. It has no three-point or overlap conclusion.

knowledge_library/t63/SOURCE_PINS.md:130:no OCR was needed.

knowledge_library/t86/SOURCE_PINS.md:10:accepted local inventory were searched on the audit date. The search was
knowledge_library/t86/SOURCE_PINS.md:11:bounded rather than claimed exhaustive.

knowledge_library/t86/SOURCE_PINS.md:27:29 (2009), 1705--1722.
knowledge_library/t86/SOURCE_PINS.md:29:- DOI: <https://doi.org/10.1017/S0143385708000898>.

knowledge_library/t86/SOURCE_PINS.md:31:  <https://www.cambridge.org/core/services/aop-cambridge-core/content/view/01225FAD40EEBC38F3AE1A5C119D0267/S0143385708000898a.pdf/div-class-title-some-effective-results-for-span-class-italic-a-span-span-class-italic-b-span-div.pdf>.
knowledge_library/t86/SOURCE_PINS.md:32:- Delivered PDF: `blmv-2009.pdf`.
knowledge_library/t86/SOURCE_PINS.md:33:- PDF SHA-256:
knowledge_library/t86/SOURCE_PINS.md:34:  `372d251b5c7c4936ab4e6b9cc6fb3af2ded2c8fe81020ad3e467843c20878e3b`.
knowledge_library/t86/SOURCE_PINS.md:35:- Theorem 1.4: printed p. 1706, PDF p. 2. It assumes multiplicatively
knowledge_library/t86/SOURCE_PINS.md:36:  independent `a,b`, `H_mu(P^N) >= rho log N`, `N>N0(a,b)`,
knowledge_library/t86/SOURCE_PINS.md:37:  `delta<=rho/20`, and nonnegative `f in C^1`; it selects
knowledge_library/t86/SOURCE_PINS.md:38:  `m=a^s b^t<N` and gives equation (1.4a).
knowledge_library/t86/SOURCE_PINS.md:39:- Corollary 1.6: printed p. 1707, PDF p. 3. It records interval proportions
knowledge_library/t86/SOURCE_PINS.md:40:  and density for a finite invariant set.
knowledge_library/t86/SOURCE_PINS.md:41:- Theorem 1.8: printed p. 1707, PDF p. 3. If `a,b` are multiplicatively
knowledge_library/t86/SOURCE_PINS.md:42:  independent and an irrational `alpha` satisfies
knowledge_library/t86/SOURCE_PINS.md:43:  `|alpha-p/q|>=q^(-k)` for every `q>=2,p in Z`, then
knowledge_library/t86/SOURCE_PINS.md:44:  `{a^s b^t alpha : s,t<=N}` is `(log log N)^(-kappa_6)`-dense for
knowledge_library/t86/SOURCE_PINS.md:45:  `N>=N0(k,a,b)`.

knowledge_library/t86/SOURCE_PINS.md:68:## S3: Hata complex-integral approximants
knowledge_library/t86/SOURCE_PINS.md:70:Masayoshi Hata, "Rational approximations to pi and some other numbers,"
knowledge_library/t86/SOURCE_PINS.md:71:*Acta Arithmetica* 63 (1993), 335--349.
knowledge_library/t86/SOURCE_PINS.md:73:- DOI: <https://doi.org/10.4064/aa-63-4-335-349>.

knowledge_library/t86/SOURCE_PINS.md:78:  `c3294d1987dfd013ec4d13f93737233177817d50c9c102ea95033e986cd9e3df`.
knowledge_library/t86/SOURCE_PINS.md:79:- Equations (1.3)--(1.4): printed p. 336, PDF p. 2, define the complex
knowledge_library/t86/SOURCE_PINS.md:80:  integral and rational function.
knowledge_library/t86/SOURCE_PINS.md:81:- Theorem 1.1: printed pp. 336--337, PDF pp. 2--3. For every epsilon>0,
knowledge_library/t86/SOURCE_PINS.md:82:  all sufficiently large `H=max(|q|,|r|)` satisfy the stated lower bound for
knowledge_library/t86/SOURCE_PINS.md:83:  `p+q*pi+r*log 2`; the exponent is `7.016045...`.
knowledge_library/t86/SOURCE_PINS.md:84:- Lemma 2.2, equations (2.2)--(2.3): printed pp. 339--340, PDF pp. 5--6,
knowledge_library/t86/SOURCE_PINS.md:85:  defines the denominator multiplier and its asymptotic exponent `kappa`.

knowledge_library/t86/SOURCE_PINS.md:87:- Printed pp. 344--345, PDF pp. 10--11: defines Gaussian integers
knowledge_library/t86/SOURCE_PINS.md:88:  `p_n,q_n,r_n`, the relation `q_n*pi-p_n=epsilon_n`, and positive constants
knowledge_library/t86/SOURCE_PINS.md:89:  `sigma,tau` with `log|q_n|/n -> sigma` and
knowledge_library/t86/SOURCE_PINS.md:90:  `log|epsilon_n|/n -> -tau`; the proof gives `sigma/tau=7.016045...`.

knowledge_library/t17/SOURCE_PINS.md:5:## Proof source
knowledge_library/t17/SOURCE_PINS.md:7:Lorenz Milla, *A detailed proof of the Chudnovsky formula with means of basic
knowledge_library/t17/SOURCE_PINS.md:8:complex analysis*, arXiv:1809.00533v6 (2021),
knowledge_library/t17/SOURCE_PINS.md:9:<https://arxiv.org/pdf/1809.00533v6>.
knowledge_library/t17/SOURCE_PINS.md:11:- Retained PDF: `milla-1809.00533v6.pdf`.
knowledge_library/t17/SOURCE_PINS.md:12:- PDF SHA-256: `69e9513d3c03c7c5c5dce12b24187b2522e0f1b08d54266a15eef93a3421cd20`.
knowledge_library/t17/SOURCE_PINS.md:13:- `pdftotext -layout` extract: `milla-1809.00533v6.txt`.
knowledge_library/t17/SOURCE_PINS.md:14:- Extract SHA-256: `edf60a42b2e1c7dc56f3f5fd5175f381860d944d38a5ee131d0815e838ce5a83`.
knowledge_library/t17/SOURCE_PINS.md:15:- Theorem 0.1 (Main Theorem 9.7), PDF/printed p. 3: the general identity,
knowledge_library/t17/SOURCE_PINS.md:16:  with the explicit hypothesis `Im(tau) > 1.25`, definitions of `J` and `s2`
knowledge_library/t17/SOURCE_PINS.md:17:  immediately above it, and the principal square-root branch stated below it.
knowledge_library/t17/SOURCE_PINS.md:18:- Theorem 10.12, PDF/printed p. 44: the exact Chudnovsky formula used here.
knowledge_library/t17/SOURCE_PINS.md:19:  Its proof specializes Main Theorem 9.7 at
knowledge_library/t17/SOURCE_PINS.md:20:  `tau_163=(1+i*sqrt(163))/2`, uses the exact `J` and `s2` values from Tables
knowledge_library/t17/SOURCE_PINS.md:21:  10.1 and 10.2, and ends by multiplying by `545140134`.
knowledge_library/t17/SOURCE_PINS.md:22:- The hypothesis is satisfied because
knowledge_library/t17/SOURCE_PINS.md:23:  `Im(tau_163)=sqrt(163)/2 > 5/4`; both sides are positive real quantities and
knowledge_library/t17/SOURCE_PINS.md:24:  the displayed square roots therefore use the positive real branch.
knowledge_library/t17/SOURCE_PINS.md:25:- Chapter 10, especially Propositions 10.2-10.4 and Theorem 10.10, supplies
knowledge_library/t17/SOURCE_PINS.md:26:  exactness of the CM values. The author's footnote on p. 3 explicitly says
knowledge_library/t17/SOURCE_PINS.md:27:  this chapter cites external literature for the facts that the relevant
knowledge_library/t17/SOURCE_PINS.md:28:  `1728J(tau)` is integral and `s2(tau)` is rational. Thus the retained paper
knowledge_library/t17/SOURCE_PINS.md:29:  is a published proof with cited inputs, not a foundationally self-contained
knowledge_library/t17/SOURCE_PINS.md:30:  formal proof.

knowledge_library/t89/SOURCE_PINS.md:27:## S1: Kempner/Fredholm number
knowledge_library/t89/SOURCE_PINS.md:29:Aubrey J. Kempner, "On Transcendental Numbers," *Transactions of the
knowledge_library/t89/SOURCE_PINS.md:30:American Mathematical Society* 17 (1916), no. 4, 476--482.
knowledge_library/t89/SOURCE_PINS.md:32:- DOI: <https://doi.org/10.1090/S0002-9947-1916-1501054-4>.
knowledge_library/t89/SOURCE_PINS.md:33:- Publisher PDF URL:
knowledge_library/t89/SOURCE_PINS.md:34:  <https://www.ams.org/journals/tran/1916-017-04/S0002-9947-1916-1501054-4/S0002-9947-1916-1501054-4.pdf>.
knowledge_library/t89/SOURCE_PINS.md:35:- File: `kempner-1916.pdf`.
knowledge_library/t89/SOURCE_PINS.md:36:- SHA-256:
knowledge_library/t89/SOURCE_PINS.md:37:  `99c4bf8d04d2dbdc63e8d274266f212072d4c248fcbc659e60ca7fa9350eb014`.
knowledge_library/t89/SOURCE_PINS.md:38:- Printed p. 477, PDF p. 2: the displayed theorem treats
knowledge_library/t89/SOURCE_PINS.md:39:  `sum alpha_n/a^(2^n) * (p/q)^n` for bounded nonzero integral
knowledge_library/t89/SOURCE_PINS.md:40:  coefficients and gives transcendence under its stated hypotheses.
knowledge_library/t89/SOURCE_PINS.md:41:- Printed p. 482, PDF p. 7: setting `p/q=1` is stated explicitly, followed
knowledge_library/t89/SOURCE_PINS.md:42:  by the Fredholm function `sum x^(2^n)` and its transcendental values.
knowledge_library/t89/SOURCE_PINS.md:43:- The PDF is a scan with an embedded approximate OCR layer. Formula locators
knowledge_library/t89/SOURCE_PINS.md:44:  were checked against the rendered page; the report uses only the displayed
knowledge_library/t89/SOURCE_PINS.md:45:  specialization `a=10`, `alpha_n=1`, `p/q=1`.
knowledge_library/t89/SOURCE_PINS.md:47:## S2: Thue--Morse recurrence and Mahler product
knowledge_library/t89/SOURCE_PINS.md:49:Dmitry Badziahin and Evgeniy Zorin, "On the Irrationality Measure of the
knowledge_library/t89/SOURCE_PINS.md:50:Thue--Morse Constant," *Mathematical Proceedings of the Cambridge
knowledge_library/t89/SOURCE_PINS.md:51:Philosophical Society* 168 (2020), 455--472.
knowledge_library/t89/SOURCE_PINS.md:53:- DOI: <https://doi.org/10.1017/S0305004118000117>.
knowledge_library/t89/SOURCE_PINS.md:54:- arXiv identifier: <https://arxiv.org/abs/1707.06677v1>.
knowledge_library/t89/SOURCE_PINS.md:55:- Primary preprint PDF URL: <https://arxiv.org/pdf/1707.06677v1>.

knowledge_library/t89/SOURCE_PINS.md:57:- SHA-256:
knowledge_library/t89/SOURCE_PINS.md:58:  `f8de296ba104cca97f4f6c3d45647e21c3db3d2207274facaf2c16b445483d15`.
knowledge_library/t89/SOURCE_PINS.md:59:- PDF pp. 3--4, equations (3)--(5): recurrence
knowledge_library/t89/SOURCE_PINS.md:60:  `t_(2n)=t_n`, `t_(2n+1)=1-t_n`, the binary constant, the product
knowledge_library/t89/SOURCE_PINS.md:61:  `f_TM(z)=product_(k>=0)(1-z^(-2^k))`, and
knowledge_library/t89/SOURCE_PINS.md:62:  `f_TM(z^2)=z/(z-1) f_TM(z)`.
knowledge_library/t89/SOURCE_PINS.md:63:- PDF pp. 7--8, Theorem 2: the stated restricted approximation theorem for
knowledge_library/t89/SOURCE_PINS.md:64:  the product family at integer arguments. T89 records it only to distinguish
knowledge_library/t89/SOURCE_PINS.md:65:  scalar approximation from the symbolic collision calculation; it is not a
knowledge_library/t89/SOURCE_PINS.md:66:  premise of that calculation.

knowledge_library/t20/SOURCE_PINS.md:14:- Extract SHA-256: `edf60a42b2e1c7dc56f3f5fd5175f381860d944d38a5ee131d0815e838ce5a83`.
knowledge_library/t20/SOURCE_PINS.md:15:- Theorem 0.1 (Main Theorem 9.7), PDF/printed p. 3: the general identity,
knowledge_library/t20/SOURCE_PINS.md:16:  with the explicit hypothesis `Im(tau) > 1.25`, definitions of `J` and `s2`
knowledge_library/t20/SOURCE_PINS.md:17:  immediately above it, and the principal square-root branch stated below it.
knowledge_library/t20/SOURCE_PINS.md:18:- Theorem 10.12, PDF/printed p. 44: the exact Chudnovsky formula used here.
knowledge_library/t20/SOURCE_PINS.md:19:  Its proof specializes Main Theorem 9.7 at
knowledge_library/t20/SOURCE_PINS.md:20:  `tau_163=(1+i*sqrt(163))/2`, uses the exact `J` and `s2` values from Tables
knowledge_library/t20/SOURCE_PINS.md:21:  10.1 and 10.2, and ends by multiplying by `545140134`.
knowledge_library/t20/SOURCE_PINS.md:22:- The hypothesis is satisfied because
knowledge_library/t20/SOURCE_PINS.md:23:  `Im(tau_163)=sqrt(163)/2 > 5/4`; both sides are positive real quantities and
knowledge_library/t20/SOURCE_PINS.md:24:  the displayed square roots therefore use the positive real branch.
knowledge_library/t20/SOURCE_PINS.md:25:- Chapter 10, especially Propositions 10.2-10.4 and Theorem 10.10, supplies
knowledge_library/t20/SOURCE_PINS.md:26:  exactness of the CM values. The author's footnote on p. 3 explicitly says
knowledge_library/t20/SOURCE_PINS.md:27:  this chapter cites external literature for the facts that the relevant
knowledge_library/t20/SOURCE_PINS.md:28:  `1728J(tau)` is integral and `s2(tau)` is rational. Thus the retained paper
knowledge_library/t20/SOURCE_PINS.md:29:  is a published proof with cited inputs, not a foundationally self-contained
knowledge_library/t20/SOURCE_PINS.md:30:  formal proof.

knowledge_library/t125/SOURCE_PINS.md:54:## S3: logarithmic odd-order correlations
knowledge_library/t125/SOURCE_PINS.md:56:- Authors: Terence Tao and Joni Teravainen.
knowledge_library/t125/SOURCE_PINS.md:57:- Title: *Odd order cases of the logarithmically averaged Chowla conjecture*.
knowledge_library/t125/SOURCE_PINS.md:58:- Version inspected: arXiv:1710.02112v1, dated 5 October 2017.
knowledge_library/t125/SOURCE_PINS.md:59:- Abstract page: <https://arxiv.org/abs/1710.02112>.
knowledge_library/t125/SOURCE_PINS.md:60:- Versioned PDF: <https://arxiv.org/pdf/1710.02112v1>.
knowledge_library/t125/SOURCE_PINS.md:61:- DOI: <https://doi.org/10.5802/jtnb.1062>.
knowledge_library/t125/SOURCE_PINS.md:62:- Delivered file: `tao-teravainen-1710.02112v1.pdf`.
knowledge_library/t125/SOURCE_PINS.md:63:- SHA-256: `232dc29917cf20f223695e3a680830e5db3b4a221049cd22f040b31144369748`.
knowledge_library/t125/SOURCE_PINS.md:64:- Exact locators: PDF p. 2, Theorem 1.1 and footnote 1; PDF p. 3,
knowledge_library/t125/SOURCE_PINS.md:65:  Remark 1.2. The order `k` is any fixed odd natural number; all affine
knowledge_library/t125/SOURCE_PINS.md:66:  coefficients are fixed before `x -> infinity`; the average is `1/log x`
knowledge_library/t125/SOURCE_PINS.md:67:  times a `1/n` weighted sum; no nondegeneracy condition is needed at odd
knowledge_library/t125/SOURCE_PINS.md:68:  order; and the moving-window normalization by `log omega(x)` is explicitly
knowledge_library/t125/SOURCE_PINS.md:69:  noted.
knowledge_library/t125/SOURCE_PINS.md:70:- Use: C-LOG.

knowledge_library/notes/t96/SOURCE_PINS.md:5:## Audit scope
knowledge_library/notes/t96/SOURCE_PINS.md:7:The bounded query class was `Stoneham` plus `Poissonian pair correlation`.
knowledge_library/notes/t96/SOURCE_PINS.md:8:The two primary sources below were inspected directly. They cover the family
knowledge_library/notes/t96/SOURCE_PINS.md:9:definition and the known `(2,3)` non-PPC theorem, respectively. This is an
knowledge_library/notes/t96/SOURCE_PINS.md:10:exact coverage audit of the two-source corpus, not an exhaustive literature
knowledge_library/notes/t96/SOURCE_PINS.md:11:search and not a novelty claim for T96.
knowledge_library/notes/t96/SOURCE_PINS.md:13:The unverified T93 note was used only to locate this corpus. Its calculations
knowledge_library/notes/t96/SOURCE_PINS.md:14:are not cited as established; the family arithmetic is independently proved in
knowledge_library/notes/t96/SOURCE_PINS.md:15:`REPORT.md`.

knowledge_library/notes/t82/SOURCE_PINS.md:72:vendored implementation, regenerates the square-root bracket and endpoints,
knowledge_library/notes/t82/SOURCE_PINS.md:73:and byte-compares the resulting endpoint file with the vendored pin.

knowledge_library/notes/t82/SOURCE_PINS.md:78:specified in `REPORT.md`, not the unweighted sum of the global Chudnovsky
knowledge_library/notes/t82/SOURCE_PINS.md:79:terms. T82 uses the proved merge identity rather than overreading that

knowledge_library/notes/t65/SOURCE_PINS.md:45:that the modular equality is incorrect when `2n+1>d-k`, with missing
knowledge_library/notes/t65/SOURCE_PINS.md:46:denominator `5^(2n+1-d+k)m`.

knowledge_library/notes/t91/SOURCE_PINS.md:88:review.  Three independent clean-context searches were run, one for
knowledge_library/notes/t91/SOURCE_PINS.md:89:Thue--Morse, one for period-doubling, and one for a carry/automatic alternative.

knowledge_library/notes/t91/SOURCE_PINS.md:104:The retained set was capped at three before drafting.  The exclusion rules were
knowledge_library/notes/t91/SOURCE_PINS.md:105:applied as follows.
knowledge_library/notes/t91/SOURCE_PINS.md:107:- Rudin--Shapiro was excluded: its standard exact polynomial/Walsh cancellation
knowledge_library/notes/t91/SOURCE_PINS.md:108:  identity is not itself a literal block-collision or successor statement and
knowledge_library/notes/t91/SOURCE_PINS.md:109:  would duplicate the T67 warning.
knowledge_library/notes/t91/SOURCE_PINS.md:110:- Champernowne/de Bruijn stage constructions were excluded as artificial
knowledge_library/notes/t91/SOURCE_PINS.md:111:  streams, duplicating rather than improving T37/T49.
knowledge_library/notes/t91/SOURCE_PINS.md:112:- Generic automatic-sequence linear complexity and entropy results were
knowledge_library/notes/t91/SOURCE_PINS.md:113:  excluded because they do not supply an exact collision calculation.
knowledge_library/notes/t91/SOURCE_PINS.md:114:- General substitution recognizability was not retained as a fourth system;
knowledge_library/notes/t91/SOURCE_PINS.md:115:  the system-specific source pins above are enough for the three calculations.

knowledge_library/notes/t99/SOURCE_PINS.md:16:periods of rational fractions with applications to normal numbers,"
knowledge_library/notes/t99/SOURCE_PINS.md:17:*Acta Arithmetica* 22 (1973), 371--389.
knowledge_library/notes/t99/SOURCE_PINS.md:19:- DOI: https://doi.org/10.4064/aa-22-4-371-389

knowledge_library/notes/t99/SOURCE_PINS.md:21:- Retrieval URL:

knowledge_library/notes/t99/SOURCE_PINS.md:23:- Local PDF: `stoneham-1973.pdf`
knowledge_library/notes/t99/SOURCE_PINS.md:24:- PDF SHA-256: `62d1718944b11b61543a20eebc2df9adbe94b94f825befa1774063897d2586d3`
knowledge_library/notes/t99/SOURCE_PINS.md:25:- Exact locator: journal p. 372, local PDF page 2, left panel, equation (1.0)
knowledge_library/notes/t99/SOURCE_PINS.md:26:  and the immediately following sentence.
knowledge_library/notes/t99/SOURCE_PINS.md:27:- Exact scope: for an odd prime `p` and primitive root `g modulo p^2`, (1.0)
knowledge_library/notes/t99/SOURCE_PINS.md:28:  defines `w(g,p)=sum_(q>=1) 1/(p^q*g^(p^q))`; the following sentence states
knowledge_library/notes/t99/SOURCE_PINS.md:29:  transcendental, non-Liouville, and normal properties.
knowledge_library/notes/t99/SOURCE_PINS.md:30:- T99 use: taking `p=3` and `g=b` identifies every base satisfying
knowledge_library/notes/t99/SOURCE_PINS.md:31:  `ord_9(b)=6` with the Stoneham family. Normality is not used.
knowledge_library/notes/t99/SOURCE_PINS.md:32:- Exclusion: this source contains no pair-correlation theorem.

1:# T153 primary-source pins
3:Audit date: 2026-08-12 UTC.
5:Exactly six unique primary PDFs were opened. The source statements below are
6:`literature-checked` against the delivered PDFs. Deductions and applicability
7:comparisons in `REPORT.md` are `proof sketch`. No OCR was needed.
9:## S1. k-Abelian locality classes
11:- Domain: symbolic collision theory.
12:- Source: Juhani Karhumaki, Aleksi Saarela, and Luca Q. Zamboni, "On a
13:  generalization of Abelian equivalence and complexity of infinite words,"
14:  *Journal of Combinatorial Theory, Series A* 120 (2013), 2189--2206.
15:- arXiv: <https://arxiv.org/abs/1301.5104v1>.
16:- DOI: <https://doi.org/10.1016/j.jcta.2013.08.008>.
17:- PDF: <https://arxiv.org/pdf/1301.5104v1>.
18:- File: `karhumaki-saarela-zamboni-1301.5104v1.pdf`.
19:- SHA-256: `2b81c55bda00622055b1d86cae93b39f6f3ad83c695d2be56af24eaa0de97ef3`.
20:- Exact locators:
21:  - Definition 2.1, PDF p. 4: `k`-Abelian equivalence means equality of all
22:    contiguous factor counts through length `k`.
23:  - Lemma 2.4(1), PDF pp. 4--5: equivalent words of equal length at most
24:    `2k-1` are equal. CORRECTION FROM T153 SKEPTIC: the following paragraph
25:    giving distinct equivalent words of length `2k` is on PDF p. 6.
26:  - Lemma 2.12, PDF p. 9: a length-`k` count vector with fixed endpoints is
27:    realizable exactly when its weighted order-`k-1` de Bruijn graph has the
28:    specified Euler path, equivalently connectivity and flow balance.
29:  - Theorem 2.14, PDF pp. 9--10: for fixed alphabet size `q>=2` and fixed `k`,
30:    the number of `k`-Abelian classes in words of length `n` is
31:    `Theta(n^(q^k-q^(k-1)))`.
32:- Scope: deterministic and pointwise, but the asymptotic constants in Theorem
33:  2.14 are only for fixed `q,k`.
35:## S2. Exact cardinalities and switchings
37:- Domain: symbolic collision theory.
38:- Source: Juhani Karhumaki, Svetlana Puzynina, Michael Rao, and Markus A.
39:  Whiteland, "On cardinalities of k-abelian equivalence classes,"
40:  *Theoretical Computer Science* 658 (2017), 190--204.
41:- arXiv: <https://arxiv.org/abs/1605.03319v1>.
42:- DOI: <https://doi.org/10.1016/j.tcs.2016.06.010>.
43:- PDF: <https://arxiv.org/pdf/1605.03319v1>.
44:- File: `karhumaki-puzynina-rao-whiteland-1605.03319v1.pdf`.
45:- SHA-256: `3367d8b31a03e0668d194913eee528f6070b79f4a43ab29ea910a19f490f4a68`.
46:- Exact locators:
47:  - Proposition 3.3, PDF p. 5: two words are `k`-Abelian equivalent exactly
48:    when connected by finitely many `k`-switchings.
49:  - Corollary 4.3, PDF p. 6: a class consists exactly of Euler paths through
50:    the weighted de Bruijn graph fixed by the length-`k` census and endpoints.
51:  - Proposition 4.7, PDF pp. 7--8: the exact class cardinality is the displayed
52:    matrix-tree determinant times the product of factorial ratios.
53:  - Proposition 5.3, PDF p. 9: a word is a `k`-Abelian singleton exactly when
54:    every ordered pair of occurring length-`k-1` factors has at most one return.
55:- Scope: deterministic and pointwise. Singleton return-uniqueness is an extra
56:  global structure condition, not a consequence of a favorable short census.
58:## S3. Logarithmic substring reconstruction
60:- Domain: symbolic collision theory / metric reconstruction.
61:- Source: Kel Levick and Ilan Shomorony, "Fundamental Limits of Multiple
62:  Sequence Reconstruction from Substrings," IEEE ISIT 2023.
63:- arXiv: <https://arxiv.org/abs/2305.05820v1>.
64:- DOI: <https://doi.org/10.1109/ISIT54713.2023.10206707>.
65:- PDF: <https://arxiv.org/pdf/2305.05820v1>.
66:- File: `levick-shomorony-2305.05820v1.pdf`.
67:- SHA-256: `6699b77376419167ee313e74bc7304acddf0166015ab68c38d685c12c036a43a`.
68:- Exact locators:
69:  - Definition 1 and Theorem 1, PDF p. 2: for `s=n^alpha` independent uniform
70:    binary length-`n` strings and `k=beta log_2 n`, reconstruction from the
71:    union of `(k+1)`-mer sets succeeds with probability tending to one when
72:    `beta>max(2alpha+1,alpha+2)` and fails with probability bounded away from
73:    zero when `beta<max(2alpha+1,alpha+3/2)`.
74:  - Lemma 1, PDF p. 2: `beta>2alpha+2` makes every `k`-mer repeat-free with
75:    probability tending to one.
76:- Scope: metric over independent uniform binary sources, not a deterministic
77:  pointwise implication and not a theorem about multiplicity-preserving
78:  decimal block counts.
80:## S4. Regular-sequence fixed points
82:- Domain: fixed-point lacunary dynamics.
83:- Source: Michael Coons, James Evans, and Neil Manibo, "Spectral Theory of
84:  Regular Sequences," *Documenta Mathematica* 27 (2022), 629--653.
85:- DOI: <https://doi.org/10.4171/DM/880>.
86:- PDF: <https://content.ems.press/assets/public/full-texts/serials/dm/27/8965811/online/10.4171-dm-880.pdf>.
87:- File: `coons-evans-manibo-10.4171-dm-880.pdf`.
88:- SHA-256: `4badf20c29df7d19695675f6aa677b7e609d763c926ff99f30123bfe29fcf034`.
89:- Exact locators: Definition 1 and Theorem 1, printed pp. 632--633; Corollary
90:  2 and equation (10), printed p. 636; Theorem 5, printed pp. 643--644.
91:- Scope: primitive finite-dimensional regular sequences, weak limit measures,
92:  exact fixed-frequency matrix recursion, and a Holder limit under a joint
93:  spectral-radius gap. There is no growing-block occupancy theorem.
94:- Duplication disposition: source, theorem tuple, and NEG-M mechanism were
95:  already inspected in T136 and copied exactly in rejected T146.
97:## S5. Nonlinear fractal Fourier decay
99:- Domain: arithmetic or fractal Fourier decay.
100:- Source: Tuomas Sahlsten and Connor Stevens, "Fourier transform and expanding
101:  maps on Cantor sets," *American Journal of Mathematics* 146 (2024).
102:- arXiv: <https://arxiv.org/abs/2009.01703>.
103:- DOI: <https://doi.org/10.1353/ajm.2024.a932433>.
104:- PDF: <https://arxiv.org/pdf/2009.01703v5>.
105:- File: `sahlsten-stevens-2009.01703.pdf`.
106:- SHA-256: `ba4878034d08a46c0e5cad13b4028922ba1ae058f0a55d11f111c4d8706693bf`.
107:- Exact locator: total nonlinearity definition (4) and Theorem 1.1, printed
108:  pp. 3--4.
109:- Scope: polynomial Fourier decay for non-atomic Gibbs measures of totally
110:  nonlinear expanding Markov maps. The linear decimal map has constant
111:  derivative cocycle and fails total nonlinearity.
112:- Duplication disposition: exact source/theorem/mechanism already appears as
113:  T104 F3 and is screened again in T150; it cannot be a T153 candidate.
115:## S6. Matrix-power character sums
117:- Domain: short structured exponential sums.
118:- Source: Alina Ostafe, Igor E. Shparlinski, and Jose Felipe Voloch,
119:  "Equations and Character Sums with Matrix Powers, Kloosterman Sums over Small
120:  Subgroups, and Quantum Ergodicity," *IMRN* 2023, 14196--14238.
121:- arXiv: <https://arxiv.org/abs/2110.10941>.
122:- DOI: <https://doi.org/10.1093/imrn/rnac226>.
123:- PDF: <https://arxiv.org/pdf/2110.10941>.
124:- File: `ostafe-shparlinski-voloch-2110.10941.pdf`.
125:- SHA-256: `4ecd0a303f6b0c93953a2df1bd011a59e88a281745dfe363865dd6ace562c934`.
126:- Exact locators: equation (2.1), arXiv PDF p. 5; Theorem 2.4 and Remark 2.6,
127:  arXiv PDF p. 6; proof in Section 6.1. CORRECTED FROM T153 SKEPTIC.
128:- Scope: complete matrix orbits over finite fields, with incomplete sums only
129:  after completion and an extra `log q` factor. At logarithmic orbit length the
130:  dimension-one specialization is larger than the trivial prefix bound.
131:- Duplication disposition: source, theorem tuple, and NEG-X mechanism were
132:  already inspected in T136 and copied exactly in rejected T146.
